Each devotee would offer you a flower, and you would offer one back in return. This was the original *guru*-*pūjā,* spontaneously emanating from their hearts. In the mood of that sweet pastime we offer you these “petals” of our endeavors.

Your servants at ISKCON Durban, South Africa.  
(written by Rukmiṇī Devī Dāsī)

## East Hartford

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept ourhumble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*viṣayā vinivartante nirāhārasya dehinaḥ*  
*rasa-varjaṁ raso ’py asya paraṁ dṛṣṭvā nivartate*

**“Though the embodied soul may be restricted from sense enjoyment, the taste for sense objects remains. But ceasing such engagements by experiencing a higher taste, he is fixed in consciousness.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our higher taste.

We know that the higher taste in this verse is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and we may have had some glimpses of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But when we think of how it was to be in your association when you were physically present on this planet, that was the highest taste we had ever experienced. And we can still experience that same higher taste of your association today. It is not any less available than when you were physically present. Any sincere devotee can associate with you at any time by reading your books, following your instructions, or hearing about your pastimes. Because you are the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON and Jagat Guru, your association is available to anyone who desires it. You are always so kind and merciful to us fallen conditioned souls. By your association, one becomes enlivened to serve Kṛṣṇa and automatically loses the taste for this material world.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to your transcendental association, and all glories to those devotees who bring other souls to your lotus feet!

Your servants at ISKCON East Hartford, Connecticut, USA  
(written by Pyāri Mohan Dāsa)

## Gainesville

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please accept my respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Ohe Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura. Chaya Vega Dami

You were my age when I was born across a distant sea.  
You kindly stretched around the world to reach lost souls like me.  
O venerable Vaiṣṇava, so I can be of service,  
Please help me shed and say farewell to these unwelcome urges:  
Farewell, O harsh and thoughtless words, propelled by foolish pride.  
You’re all I have been hearing on your domineering ride.  
Sweet Kṛṣṇa-talk exposes you as ugly, stale, and tart,  
Since Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved servant stole away my heart.  
Farewell, O egocentric mind; you now shall be in check.  
Though likely you will stick around, a monkey on my neck,  
No longer shall I labor for the projects that you start—  
Lord Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved servant stole away my heart.  
Farewell, O foolish anger, with your microscopic fuse.  
Your older brother lust is also straightaway excused.  
You stay where you are useful, like a horse before a cart,  
Since Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved servant stole away my heart.  
Farewell, O morbid, rotten food the common man consumes,  
Unending, luckless corpses on his dinner plate of doom.  
What pleases Kṛṣṇa’s taste buds is my culinary art  
Since Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved servant stole away my heart.  
Farewell, O belly’s pushings. Your demands exceed the space  
Required to keep the soul and body properly in place.  
Your schemes no longer seem important, valuable, or smart  
Since Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved servant stole away my heart.  
Farewell, O mighty, lurid pull of fleshly, scented globes,  
Half-hidden by the dazzle of this year’s designer clothes.  
You’ve titillated me too long; it’s time for us to part,  
Since Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved servant stole away my heart.  
Farewell, O aspirations for a kingdom in this world,  
A vict’ry flag of happiness that never quite unfurled.  
Vṛndāvan is my target now; I’m ready to depart,  
For Kṛṣṇa’s most beloved servant stole away my heart.  
Do kindly, saintly persons steal? In this case, it is so,  
For though you are so subtle that one doesn’t really know,  
Your words continue, Prabhupāda, to cause so many thefts,  
Soon everyone shall lose the useless urge for birth and death.

Your servants at Kṛṣṇa House, ISKCON Gainesville, Florida, USA.  
(written by Kālakaṇṭha Dāsa)

## Gītā-nāgarī

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your sweet lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā!

In Gītā-nāgarī, on July 15th, 1976, you gave an evening lecture later entitled “Living as Kṛṣṇa Lived.” You said:

But if we keep ourselves within the village and properly organize as Kṛṣṇa personally showed us the example, He was personally taking care of cows, calves. When He was a child, He was taking care of the calves. When He was grown up, He was taking care of the cows, although He was a very rich man’s son: Nanda Mahārāja was the village king. But still, Kṛṣṇa was working hard the whole day, going in the morning to the pasturing grounds and coming back in the evening. Thus He used to take His bath, change dress, and immediately go to sleep. This was Kṛṣṇa’s pastime. So we should follow His footprints. Live village life, simple life, and be satisfied with the bare necessities. There is no need of luxury. And save time and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. This is ideal life. So, I am very pleased that you are doing that, and do it more nicely so that others may be attracted. So this is an ideal village. Develop it more and more. Do it very nicely. I am very happy to see things are going nicely. Thank you very much.

You further expressed many desires and clear instructions in your famous *Gītā*-nāgarī prophecy (contained in some 1956 BTG articles). Our fervent prayer is that you kindly bestow upon us the necessary qualifications to fulfill your desires, your prophecy.

As we reflect on the many ways we need to intensify our commitment to your mission on this earthly plane, and in particular your mission for *Gītā*-nāgarī, we realize how far we fall short. We realize how much more there is to do to gain your ultimate favor. You still remain merciful, however, and we continue like children who take for granted the protection of their parents, remaining oblivious and familiar yet always receiving care and love.

So our humble prayer to you this year, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is to please help us become more aware of your mercy and favor on us so that we may start to feel heartfelt gratitude, which will propel us into active, focused, steady, loving service. We especially pray for increased determination to read your books and chant Kṛṣṇa’s holy name with sincerity and love. The hidden jewels in your books sever the many deep-seated, complex shackles of conditioning. Your books rain down refreshing showers on our parched hearts, allowing some seeds of *bhakti* to germinate. Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, truly nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa, is ours for the taking. Please help us associate with and worship Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara by chanting and hearing Their holy names with hearts filled with love, humility, and devotion.

You have patiently and lovingly provided so much for us here in *Gītā*-nāgarī. First and foremost are Their most merciful and beautiful Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara, so divinely interactive. In addition, there are the gentle, selfless cows and the fertile, bio-diverse land—so much opulence. And of course there is the community of *bhakti* practitioners, all of them sincere and determined in their efforts to please you. From our youngest to our eldest family members, everyone is so sweet, unique, talented, and special. In addition, you have given us an ocean of well-wishers: devotees who actively support and encourage your goals for *Gītā*-nāgarī. Without hesitation and with generosity they offer their time, energy, and resources, embracing in the core of their hearts your instructions and desires. What good fortune, a rare privilege, and actually our real shelter, to serve Your Divine Grace with all of these great souls—our community devotees and well-wishers—and all simply for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara!

We come before you this year with desperate pleas to kindly remove any obstacle that stands in the way of your plans here for *Gītā*-nāgarī. The biggest and most difficult obstacle is our own false ego. Please, please, please help us overcome this most difficult impediment to *bhakti.* It incarcerates us mercilessly, it bewilders us, and ultimately it closes us off from the free-flowing mercy of the *paramparā*. And in time, whatever successes you allow, please let us truly understand that they are all a result of your blessings, that they are all to your credit, and that they are all due to the sweet will of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara. Please take away this awful tendency we have, to think ourselves the controllers and doers, and please allow us to serve you with sincerity, faith, determination, and surrender.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, ultimately our only delight is to see you pleased. Please help us remember this.

Every day when we turn our attention outside of our *Gītā*-nāgarī realm, interacting with the global ISKCON village on cyberspace, we read how your glories continue to become manifest. While there are always challenges and obstacles, everything is expanding phenomenally, all due to your supreme purity and devotion and your ardent desire to bestow mercy on the fallen, conditioned souls of this age.

How can we ever repay you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Please bless us to serve you as a united and harmonious family, and to give everything, ultimately even this life we so foolishly cling to, for the pleasure of you and your beloved Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara.

We offer you our prostrated obeisances repeatedly.

With all our love and gratitude,  
Your children, grandchildren, and great-grandchildren at ISKCON’s Gītā-nāgarī farm community in Pennsylvania, USA.

## Guyana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we find it a great challenge to properly describe your glorious attributes, which are as numerous as the waves of the ocean. On this most blissful and blessed day, we servants of yours in the *Guyana yātrā* ponder your transcendental words and activities. By so doing we become purified of material contamination, thus fueling our desire to become more sheltered under your lotus feet, which are the most secure place to be.

Every single aspect of your existence is magnificent and able to arouse the dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the heart of those who are struggling in material existence. You compassionately established your ISKCON society to benefit everyone, especially your followers, and ISKCON will go down in history as the most beneficial society on the face of the earth. Therefore those who are astute shall understand that Your Divine Grace is the world’s foremost well-wisher. By your mercy, which purifies the hearts of the lost conditioned souls, you are elevating unfortunate, hopeless, and condemned persons all over the world through your ISKCON society, which is rapidly growing even in remote places like Guyana. Lord Kṛṣṇa sent Your Divine Grace to this planet to execute the mission of Lord Caitanya.

Perfectly pleasing Lord Caitanya depends on our perfectly surrendering to your mission. Through dedication to your movement, one spontaneously and delightfully becomes decorated with renunciation of material sense gratification, which binds us to material existence. To please you, we in the Guyana *yātrā* emphasize preaching the message of Kṛṣṇa and glorifying you. Each day we try as much as we can to rededicate our lives to fulfilling your mission, and in our own little way we thus try to relieve your anxiety by saving those in our *prabhu-datta deśa*. Therefore we beg you to please give us the necessary vigor and eagerness to dedicate our service, our offerings, and our very lives to your mission.

The *śāstra* says that the relationship between the spiritual master and the disciple is not hindered by the limitation of time and space, nor diminished by physical absence, nor subject to forgetfulness or change, but rather it is eternally existent and infinitely increasing. Our relationship with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, need not be limited to fading memories that may induce us to cross the thin line between genuine sentiment and sentimentality, but rather we want to continually deepen our understanding of your instructions so we may better please you. Please bless us that somehow we can maintain enthusiasm and sincerity and strong appreciation for your mission.

As beginners, we could not fully appreciate your greatness and the worldwide extent of your service to the cause of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa. And of course we still cannot begin to grasp the degree of the purity of your devotion or how your powerful preaching is changing the course of the world. A unique kind of appreciation, however, does awaken within us when we take shelter of your instructions and remain steadfast in the devotional service you have prescribed. Our real link with you is through those instructions, when we fully dedicate ourselves to attempting to fulfill your desires and orders.

The continuing growth of ISKCON in our country—the increasing book and BTG distribution, devotee-making and -training, festivals, and *prasādam* distribution—is clear evidence of your continued presence. We realize that none of this could go on, what to speak of increase, if it were not for the continuous inspiration and guidance you impart to us. We see all our small achievements as glorification of you and evidence of the truth of your timeless instructions.

We constantly pray that we can remember you always as the eternal source of all our abilities, knowledge, and devotion. Today we pray that we have the power to use our every breath, word, and deed to spread your mission. Our lives already belong to you, since it is you who have given us a life. Therefore what other course do we have but to utterly surrender it all to you? There is nothing else to wish for.

We pray that we will never become illusioned and that you will give us the transcendental vision to remain fixed in your mission. We know you are with us because you are manifest in the hearts and activities of your most sincere followers. Let your personal example inspire us to give up our false pride, mental concoctions, and selfish desires and simply work to fulfill your mission of bringing everyone to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

On this most auspicious and blessed day we fervently pray to you for guidance and blessings so that we will be able to give up the desires for all forms of sense gratification and surrender ourselves fully at your lotus feet. May we unflinchingly execute the orders of your duly appointed representatives so that your divine glories may be heard and sung in every town and village in Guyana.

Although we are unqualified, out of your kindness you have accepted us as your granddisciples, and you are guiding us toward the perfection of life, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your appearance in this world is the most significant event, an inexhaustible source of illumination defeating the dense darkness of Kali-yuga. We make this humble offering to glorify you on this anniversary of your appearance in this world, and we pray that we may always be engaged in your service.

We beg to always remain  
Your insignificant servants in Guyana.  
(written by Paramātmā Dāsa)

## ISKCON of Silicon Valley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in dust of your lotus feet.

When we ask ourselves, “How does the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva walk in this world?” we can only answer, “Exactly as you have walked, Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

Śrīla Dvaipāyana Vyāsadeva accepts anxiety and inconvenience simply to benefit and reclaim the souls who have fallen into illusion (*māyā*). He divides and repackages the Vedic message to attract the people of Kali-yuga and save them from suffering.

Today we worship you as the perfect representative of Śrīla Vedavyāsa because we see how you’ve followed his path by compiling your beautiful books—perfectly in line with his teachings—so that people of Kali-yuga might achieve the ultimate benefit.

While going door-to-door to sell your books, following your instructions to do good for others, we know that we are also perfectly following the path of Vyāsadeva.

The apartments and homes that we approach are basically containers in which people keep their things: food, pets, plants, clothes, pictures, furniture, electronics, family members, and so on. Inside they cook, eat, read, watch television, work before computer screens, talk on the telephone, sleep, and breed.

Many houses are so full of junk that it is difficult to understand how the occupants can move about inside. Some residences emit disgusting odors; others have porches strewn with cigarette butts or old newspapers that nobody ever bothers to pick up. Others have dogs inside that bark, growl, and jump up on the inside of the front door when we knock. Still other dwellings are neat and tidy (but these are rare). Some homes are like small palaces with swimming pools, tennis courts, sprawling lawns, well-kept gardens, and luxurious entryways.

However, regardless of the state of any of these lodgings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that unless they contain at least one of your books, every home is no better than an oversized jail or a gigantic coffin.

Lord *Brahmā* says, “My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, until people become Your devotees, their material attachments and desires remain thieves, their homes remain prisons, and their affectionate feelings for their family members remain foot-shackles.” (*Śrīmad*-*Bhāgavatam* 10.14.36)

As we visit these homes and apartments and knock upon their doors, the residents open up to see what we want; and they quickly learn that we want them to take your books into their homes. And when a fortunate householder accepts and places your books inside, his or her home becomes sanctified.

In the *Skanda Purāṇa* there is this statement: “A person who is constantly engaged in reading literature enunciating the cultivation of Vaiṣṇava devotional service is always glorious in human society, and certainly Lord Kṛṣṇa becomes pleased with him. A person who very carefully keeps such literature at home and offers respectful obeisances to it becomes freed from all sinful reactions and ultimately becomes worshipable by the demigods.” \[*The Nectar of Devotion,* chapter 12\]

Despite the many benefits of accepting your books, some people hesitate. When we present your books, people sometimes say, “No time!” “No space!” We take this as a metaphor for the conditioned souls, who are so full of material desires that they have no space in their hearts to accommodate Kṛṣṇa or even a moment to remember Him.

You wrote about this same phenomenon when you personally distributed your *Back to Godhead* magazine in India, before coming to America. In one article entitled “No Time: a Chronic Disease of the Common Man,” you write, “When we approach some gentleman and request him to become a reader of *Back to Godhead, s*ometimes we hear the reply, ‘NO TIME.’”

While introducing your books, as we remember how you heard the same mantra, “No time,” we feel close to you. But we are never discouraged by these “no times” because we know that each one brings us closer to that fortunate soul who will accept your books and receive Kṛṣṇa’s special favor.

Regarding this special favor, in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī describes how before the appearance of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu an elevated Vaiṣṇava named Guṇarāja Khān, from Kulīna-grāma, wrote a book in simple Bengali prose entitled *Kṛṣṇa-vijaya,* in which he glorifies Kṛṣṇa. Although the author’s verses did not technically meet high poetical standards, because the book contained expressions of pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu accepted it as sublime. In fact, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, charmed by the scent of pure devotion emanating from Guṇarāja Khān’s book, said the following:

“Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda Mahārāja is my life and soul.” By these words \[from the book *Kṛṣṇa-vijaya*\] I have been sold into the hands of the descendants of Guṇarāja Khān. \[*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 15.99\]

What’s more, while addressing the residents of Kulīna-grāma—the descendants of Guṇarāja Khān—Lord Caitanya added, “To say nothing of you, even a dog living in your village is very dear to Me. What, then, to speak of others?” In other words, because Guṇarāja Khān, a native of Kulīna-grāma, had expressed sentiments of pure devotion in his book, Lord Caitanya blessed not only him but also his descendants and anyone else who lived in Kulīna-grāma—even the dogs.

Pure devotion is uniquely fragrant, and that fragrance is so appealing that it attracts even Kṛṣṇa, who is known to be completely self-satisfied. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* confirms this:

As the chariot of air carries an aroma from its source and immediately catches the sense of smell, similarly, one who constantly engages in devotional service, in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, can catch the Supreme Soul, who is equally present everywhere. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.29.20\]

What to speak of attracting the Supreme Lord, the sweet scent of devotion coming from the mouth of a pure devotee when he or she speaks compels and transforms ordinary living entities. Pṛṭhu Mahārāja verifies this as follows:

My dear Lord, You are glorified by the selected verses uttered by great personalities. Such glorification of Your lotus feet is just like saffron particles. When the transcendental vibration from the mouths of great devotees carries the aroma of the saffron dust of Your lotus feet, the forgetful living entity gradually remembers his eternal relationship with You. Devotees thus gradually come to the right conclusion about the value of life. My dear Lord, I therefore do not need any other benediction but the opportunity to hear from the mouth of Your pure devotee. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.20.25\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books contain the concentrated fragrance of your pure devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa. And that fragrance is spreading throughout the world, attracting the spiritual senses of living entities on every continent.

Cāṇakya Paṇḍita says that the aroma of one extraordinary flower can refresh a whole forest. In the same way, the scent of pure devotion emanating from the heart of a pure devotee can reform the entire world.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* describes how Dhruva Mahārāja, as he practiced *aṣṭāṅga*-*yoga,* suspended his breathing, thus stopping the breathing of everyone in the universe, including the demigods. In your purport to this verse, you write:

This example of Dhruva Mahārāja’s closing the holes of his personal body and thereby closing the breathing holes of the total universe clearly indicates that a devotee, by his personal devotional service, can influence all the people of the whole world to become devotees of the Lord. If there is only one pure devotee in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he can change the total consciousness of the world into Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is not very difficult to understand if we study the behavior of Dhruva Mahārāja. \[*Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* 4.8.80, purport\]

By your influence, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, this year not only did ISV move into a beautiful new temple, but the devotees here also deepened their practice of hearing and chanting, improved the standards of Deity worship, and expanded their various preaching programs, and many have taken initiation vows.

We thank you for bringing us the fragrance of pure devotional service and pray that you continue to empower us to help you spread it throughout the world.

Your eternal servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.  
(written by Vaiśeṣika Dāsa)

## Khasadeśa

Our dear spiritual grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to your ISKCON movement and your faithful followers!

It seems that over the years we have had a tendency to overlook your personal presence and develop more attachment to our gurus, who are constantly giving us so much inspiring instruction and encouragement in our service. They are like fathers to us. They give us warmth and nourishment for our spiritual growth. In your case, however, although we have seen video of your pastimes, heard your instructions, and read your books, we never had your personal association and training in life. Thus we have felt a little distant from you. Our vision has been that you are the spiritual pillar of ISKCON, the worshipable founder-*ācārya,* the spiritual guide of countless Vaiṣṇava followers, and the perfect example of our Kṛṣṇa conscious practices. We wish now to more and more feel that you are also our affectionate grandfather who are always present in our lives.

Who would have thought that one day hundreds of Chinese of all ages and walks of life would take up the process of devotional service and assist you in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this godforsaken land, where people are constantly absorbed in material prosperity and sense gratification? The pure devotee always thinks of the welfare of others, even the most lowly. Therefore you have not forgotten us. Your books, your vision, your attachment to serving the *guru-paramparā*—all manifest in so many Chinese hearts. We beg at your lotus feet to give us the intelligence to bring many more hearts to the chanting of the holy names and the practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This may take many lifetimes, but we are willing to stay here and help you until everyone has received your mercy.

We devotees of the Chinese *yātrā* have come to realize more and more that to present *bhakti*-*yoga* to the mainstream Chinese society, which suffers so much from a lack of spiritual guidance, we must clearly understand the principles of your mission so that we can advance in devotional service and develop the intelligence Kṛṣṇa has given us. We are so fortunate to have that opportunity, and so grateful to you for coming to save us. Your dear and dedicated disciples also play an important role in keeping you very present in such a moving way. Our hearts swell with appreciation and admiration whenever we hear that when you began your movement at the advanced age of seventy you single-handedly did everything—writing books, giving lectures, leading *kīrtanas,* raising your disciples like your own children, cooking feasts, distributing *prasādam,* and keeping everything clean. At the same time, you were always free of pride, describing yourself as an eternal servant of your spiritual master and Lord Kṛṣṇa. Your example teaches us that we should always remember our own eternal position as menial and humble servants, no matter what service we perform.

Every morning as we sit at your lotus feet to chant *japa* we feel that you are personally present. We always strive to chant with attention and love, as you have shown us. This is the only safe place to be in this world and in the next. We therefore pray to always remain at your lotus feet.

Standing in front of you today, we gaze at your eyes. They express conviction and determination, and also, we feel, your approval and blessings for the healthy growth of ISKCON, which includes us, your little children here in China. Please guide us so that we won’t deviate from the right path.

Dear spiritual grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to understand your transcendence and intelligence more and more. We also pray for new opportunities to serve the Vaiṣṇavas, and for as much dedication to the *saṅkīrtana* mission as our spiritual masters have. Finally, we pray that you will mercifully protect the steady development of your Chinese mission by inspiring all the devotees here to work together with mutual respect, understanding, cooperation, and tolerance.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!  
Your grandchildren in Kasadeśa.

## Koundinyapur

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Yesterday we observed the holy appearance day of Bhagavān *Śrī* Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and today we are observing the holy appearance day of our ISKCON founder-*ācārya,* *Śrī* Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Kṛṣṇa sent His own man, His very intimate devotee *Śrīla Prabhupāda,* to this world just after the anniversary of His appearance day.

On the day commemorating Nanda Mahārāja’s arranging a great festival in honor of the appearance of his beloved son *Śrī* Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa’s very intimate devotee appeared. Why does a pure Vaiṣṇava come here? Kṛṣṇa’s own men are always with Him in His *sac-cid-ānanda-maya* *dhāma*. And when He sends one of them to this realm, such a Vaiṣṇava sees the relationship of everybody and everything to Kṛṣṇa. We all have a perfect loving relationship with Kṛṣṇa, eternally. A completely Kṛṣṇa conscious person sees that dormant relationship within all conditioned souls. It pains him to see all these souls forgetful of his beloved Kṛṣṇa, the Lord of the heart. Such a *prema*-saturated Vaiṣṇava cannot tolerate the *jīvas’* suffering in separation from his beloved Kṛṣṇa. He knows that if they develop Kṛṣṇa consciousness, they will no longer suffer and will go back home, back to Godhead.

Our beloved *Śrīla Prabhupāda* is such a Vaiṣṇava. Kṛṣṇa sent Him, especially empowering him to inculcate Kṛṣṇa consciousness into the hearts of the conditioned souls. *Śrīla Prabhupāda*’s spiritual master ordered him to go to the Western world and preach the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in English. The Western world is full of gross materialists, devoid of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. So who could have induced them to take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness except one who was extraordinarily empowered by the Lord?

So there is no doubt that Śrīla Prabhupāda was empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa, Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and all the previous *ācāryas*, especially Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Thus in a very short time, in the last portion of his life, he went to the Western world and fulfilled the prediction of Caitanya Mahāprabhu: *pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grām sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*: “In every town and village on this earth the glories of My name will be chanted.” In just twelve years he traveled to all corners of the world a dozen times and preached the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As a result we see that thousands of gross materialists have developed Kṛṣṇa consciousness and are now engaged in the loving service of Kṛṣṇa.

The society Śrīla Prabhupāda set up, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, is meant to be a society of true Vaiṣṇavas, devotees who are free of envy. As aforementioned, the true Vaiṣṇava sees the relationship of everyone and everything with Kṛṣṇa, who is the object of his love. In the heart of such a Vaiṣṇava, how can there be envy? In this regard Śrīla Prabhupāda set the example for his followers by his own activities.

We devotees here at ISKCON Koundinyapur are trying to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda under the guidance of His Holiness Lokanāth Swami. We have been granted five acres of land from the government of Maharashtra. This place has a vital spiritual history, Koundinyapur being the birthplace of Rukmiṇī Devī and the place where Lord Kṛṣṇa kidnapped her. The ancient Ambā Devī Temple marks the presence of Rukmiṇī in Koundinyapur. The Rukmiṇī-Viṭhṭhala temple is also situated in this holy place. We are engaging in several devotional programs, including *prasādam* and book distribution, Deity worship, cow protection, and the Sunday Festival.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers at ISKCON Koundinyapur, India.

## Las Vegas

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, O greatest savior of the world,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in dust of your lotus feet.

Thank you so much for once again giving me the privilege of writing you an offering for your glorious Vyāsa-pūjā. Since your last Vyāsa-pūjā, many wonderful things have happened in Las Vegas by dint of your divine mercy. In my last offering I included a brief report of our preaching activities here, and I also prayed to you for blessings and mercy so that we might render more and better service to your lotus feet. You have kindly listened to our prayers and fulfilled all of them! Thus, by your divine mercy we have received a wonderful facility and a tremendous opportunity to multiply all our services in Las Vegas for the pleasure of you and Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you! Thank you so much!

We all are eternally indebted to your lotus feet for everything you have done on this earth planet to save the conditioned souls of this Kali-yuga, the age of degradation. You sacrificed yourself completely to deliver the world, single-handedly starting your ISKCON society at the advanced age of seventy. You once said that to establish ISKCON you had “walked through fire” just to “give people an opportunity to associate with those who have not forgotten Kṛṣṇa.”

Desiring to facilitate the spiritual lives of generations of your followers, you spoke to them in hundreds of recorded lectures and conversations, wrote dozens of books and hundreds of letters, established over a hundred temples, installed dozens of Deities, founded a social system, instituted systematic Vaiṣṇava education, directed self-sufficiency projects, and formed an institution with a suitable hierarchy to perpetuate the *saṅkīrtana* movement into the 21st century and beyond.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura had an idea of preaching in the West, Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura organized his preaching movement to expand the ideas of his illustrious father, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, implemented an organized preaching movement in the West and all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the ideal *ācārya* for the Iron Age, and you said that your books will be “the law books for the next ten thousand years.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the embodiment of knowledge! When you were personally present, you exemplified the teachings of the *śāstras* and our predecessors *ācāryas*, and thus your disciples had first-hand experience of the practical application of your self-effulgent teachings.

There is no example to parallel that of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the perfect *goṣṭhy-ānandī,* *jagat-guru,* and *ācārya*. The risks you took are legendary, and were beyond the call of duty, even for great transcendentalists. Under the order of your spiritual master you took, as you said, “the responsibility of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western world.” For this reason you left your family to live alone and prepare yourself for this supreme mission, given to you by your spiritual master. Leaving the transcendental shelter of Vṛndāvana, you crossed the ocean and suffered several heart attacks, the taunts of uncultured Westerners, the toll of world travel in your seventies, the conspiracies of Godbrothers, the immaturity of disciples, and many other trials. Your story is told in various biographies, all proving that no one ever took greater risks for the mission of the Lord than you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, many of your godbrothers were highly exalted souls, but they never took Lord Caitanya’s order to spread the holy name throughout the world as seriously as you did. Some didn’t want to risk associating with Westerners, some didn’t want to risk dying outside Vraja or Māyāpur. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, took these risks and many others to fulfill the order of your spiritual master and the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. As you wrote:

So far I am concerned, I always take risk for Krishna. I came here in your country risking my life. And still, although I am physically unfit, I am trying to execute the orders of my Guru Maharaja as far as possible. \[Letter to Gargamuni, 12 February 1968\]

In this regard you acted exactly like Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who displayed the perfect example of an ideal devotee by showing unlimited and unflinching faith in His guru. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the personification of this highest principle of spiritual progress. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 7.95–96) we find these words of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu:

I firmly believe in these words of My spiritual master, and therefore I always chant the holy name of the Lord, alone and in the association of devotees. That holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa sometimes causes Me to chant and dance, and therefore I chant and dance. Please do not think that I intentionally do it. I do it automatically.

And in the purport you explain:

A person who cannot keep his faith in the words of his spiritual master but acts independently never receives the authority to chant the holy name of the Lord. It is said in the *Vedas* (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.23):

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

This Vedic injunction is very important, and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu supported it by His personal behavior. Believing in the words of His spiritual master, He introduced the *saṅkīrtana* movement, just as the present Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was started with belief in the words of our spiritual master. He wanted to preach, we believed in his words and tried somehow or other to fulfill them, and now this movement has become successful all over the world. Therefore faith in the words of the spiritual master and in the Supreme Personality of Godhead is the secret of success.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in actuality your entire life is a perfect, self-illuminating example of an ideal disciple, a dedicated servant and pure devotee of guru and Kṛṣṇa. You are an ideal *goṣṭhy-ānandi,* one who is not satisfied simply to become perfect himself but wants to see others also take advantage of the holy name of the Lord and advance in spiritual life.

You are the personification of pure selflessness, compassion, and anxiety over the plight of others. Therefore, you are an ideal *ācārya*. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 3.98) we find this description of an *ācārya*:

An *ācārya* does not exploit his followers. Since the *ācārya* is a confidential servitor of the Lord, his heart is always full of compassion for humanity in its suffering. He knows that all suffering is due to the absence of devotional service to the Lord, and therefore he always tries to find ways to change people’s activities, making them favorable for the attainment of devotion. That is the qualification of an *ācārya*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, another of the manifold outstanding traits you displayed as founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON is your extraordinary spiritual strength. This is readily evident from your long list of achievements in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They have no equal in the history of *Gauḍīya* Vaiṣṇavism or of humanity at large. As you wrote in your purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta Madhya* 24.277: “Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura said that the position of a Vaiṣṇava can be tested by seeing how good a touchstone he is—that is, by seeing how many Vaiṣṇavas he has made during his life.”

Your preaching has breathed new life into Lord Caitanya’s *sampradāya* because you have clarified the points of *siddhānta,* the essential conclusion of spiritual science, and distinctly elucidated the preaching mission. In your purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* *Madhya* 25.9 you write:

In this Age of Kali, real religious propaganda should induce people to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. This is possible for someone who is especially empowered by Kṛṣṇa. No one can do this without being especially favored by Kṛṣṇa.

Consequently, nowadays many souls are becoming inspired to adopt the path of Lord Caitanya, to the extent that all over the world *harināma* *saṅkīrtana* is becoming more and more popular, even among ordinary people, and is being celebrated at various events like Bhakti Fest and Kirtan Fest.

Even in Las Vegas, a place known all over the world as Sin City, by your mercy and that of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa incredible events are taking place, and *kīrtana* is becoming more and more popular. Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of your blessings and the blessings of your representatives, last year’s BTG article about our Las Vegas preaching efforts so much inspired one of your wonderful followers, Rādhā Jīvana Prabhu, that he purchased a beautiful property for Lord Kṛṣṇa’s temple in Las Vegas. We are now building a gorgeous new temple here, the first official ISKCON center in Las Vegas and, indeed, in the entire state of Nevada.

To collect funds for this project we have organized a few Kṛṣṇa conscious festivals that have attracted more of the Indian community to your ISKCON institution, and they have demonstrated a willingness to support the new temple. We have also tremendously increased the distribution of your books and the incidence of *harināma* on the fabled Las Vegas Strip. In addition, a new *bhakta* has joined your temple, and we have also increased educational programs for the congregation in general. Soon we will hold the first Holi Festival of Colors in Las Vegas, a festival that has quickly become one of the biggest and most popular Hare Kṛṣṇa festivals in America.

Our humble desire reflects exactly what you said in a lecture at Ahmedabad on 9 December 1972: “So we, our business is to become a little recognized by Kṛṣṇa. So we shall go on preaching like this at all risk.”

Therefore, dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, following your perfect example, we beg you to bless us all with unflinching and unlimited faith in guru and Kṛṣṇa. May we always be enthusiastically take any and all risks to preach in Las Vegas for your pleasure and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s. Please bless us that we will always remember Lord Kṛṣṇa in all of our activities and never, even for a moment, forget Him. Please bless us that in all circumstances we will depend on the mercy of your lotus feet and the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your humble and ever grateful servants in Las Vegas, Nevada, USA.  
(written by Surapāla Dāsa)

## Lenasia (South Africa)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humbles obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most glorious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, we contemplate the numerous blessings you have bestowed upon us. Throughout the years we have always felt your presence and guidance. We have not always been successful in accomplishing what you require from us, but we are making an earnest attempt.

Over the last few years we have tried to live by these words of yours:

Simply by associating with a pure devotee, one becomes wonderfully advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. *Sādhu-saṅga,* or association with a devotee, means always engaging in Kṛṣṇa consciousness by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra and by acting for Kṛṣṇa. Specifically, chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra purifies one, and this chanting is therefore recommended by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. *Ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam:* by chanting the names of Kṛṣṇa, the mirror of the heart is cleansed, and the devotee loses interest in everything external. When one is influenced by the external energy of the Lord, his heart is impure. When one’s heart is not pure, he cannot see how things are related to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. *Idaṁ hi viśvaṁ bhagavān ivetaraḥ* (SB 1.5.20). He whose heart is purified can see that the whole cosmic manifestation is but the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but he whose heart is contaminated sees things differently. Therefore by *sat-saṅga,* or association with devotees, one becomes perfectly pure in heart. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.59, purport\]

We try to make Your Divine Grace our focal point, and in turn all your devotees a focal point, of our service. We see part of our service as providing pure association to uplift the spirits of the devotees, and we work together to increase the influence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, not only for the immediate circle of devotees but also for society at large.

It hasn’t been easy, but when has a mammoth task come easily? The service is hard but the experience is blissful. We have seen the blissful results increase as we strive to work better together—empowering and supporting one another, challenging and motivating one another. Over the last year and a half we have had more success in this endeavor due to the formation of the ISKCON Lenasia Operations Committee (ILOC). A select group of devotees came together and have endeavored to embody the spirit of *sādhu-saṅga*. We have uplifted the quality of our activities so that we are now closer to being able to provide what you require from us. We are determined to work harder and smarter to enhance your Society, based on the principles of unity and keeping Kṛṣṇa in the center.

In a letter to Tribhuvanātha Dāsa you wrote this on 17 July 1971:

I am also glad to note that a meeting of all the presidents was held just recently in order to unify the temples. That is the process. Unless we work cooperatively, how this movement will go on? So my request to you all is that you maintain that spirit of cooperation always and help me to deliver Lord Caitanya’s message all over the globe.

In our relatively small community of devotees in Lenasia, we of the ILOC are uniting to deliver that message. We have a group of devotees who set aside personal goals for a collective goal, who co-operate to achieve the collective goal, who help one another achieve the collective goal, and who work with love for Your Divine Grace as the pillar to accomplish it all. We want to be a single unit for a single purpose. Recent results are as follows:

Consistent action based strategic and operational meetings.

A sense of collective success versus individual glory.

A sense of honoring one another in the role of servants in your movement, recognizing other devotees’ accomplishments, and supporting one another through difficulties.

Formation of key departments and working to achieve interdepartmental goals.

The outcomes have been relatively modest, but they are stepping stones to bigger things to come. Highlights include:

1\. a greater support structure for achieving departmental goals

2\. improved observance of festivals

3\. more outreach events

4\. a consistent and highly successful BYS program

5\. consistent, targeted, and highly professional seminars to educate devotees

6\. more books distributed in dynamic, professional, and expansive ways

7\. consistent BTG and donor engagement

8\. a dedicated Food for Life program, accompanied by book distribution

9\. greater exposure in media, online, and direct communication channels

10\. a plan for a better temple environment

11\. an active, targeted outreach program going beyond traditional preaching channels and crossing both racial and demographic divides

12\. Deity worship continuing even with so many unavoidable changes and challenges

13\. a plan to take care of our devotees better

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, the devotees in the ILOC have every intention of following your instructions; we just need the fuel of your love and the benediction of your causeless mercy to do so. Then the spirit the ILOC group has developed will spill over into the greater devotee community and finally society at large. This cultural change will be an offering of love to you. After all, you did the same, only the magnitude of the change you effected was massive, far beyond what we can imagine accomplishing, and we see that the whole world has been influenced by your contribution to the Kṛṣṇa consciousness revolution. Please bless us that we can accomplish a tiny fraction of what you did. Give us the strength to be brave and confident in the execution of Lord Caitanya’s mission as we face great challenges. Please help us tolerate the obstacles we face and overcome them every day as we strive to serve Your Divine Grace and work co-operatively.

Thank you for all the love you have shown us. Thank you for allowing us to be in your movement.

Your servants at ISKCON Lenasia, South Africa.

## London (Soho)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

We offer our prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you and your ISKCON movement!

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us this amazing opportunity to perfect our lives in such a wonderful and exciting way.

There is never a dull moment in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You have opened our eyes to the fresh unfolding of the Lord’s causeless mercy at every moment. You have given us the gift of understanding the foundation of everything. You have helped us see who we are and understand our eternal relationship with the Supreme Lord. In essence, you have given us completeness.

We here in London are doing our best to care for your most beloved Rādhā-Londonīśvara. We face new challenges every day, and in many ways I am sure we fail you, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*. We truly want to be able to offer you everything that will bring you and Kṛṣṇa the most pleasure.

We are trying to focus on caring for the devotees who so wonderfully dedicate their lives to your mission, and in so doing we are realizing just how difficult it is to be selfless, tolerant, and humble. It is not so easy to be the servant of the servant, yet you, Śrīla Prabhupāda— such an exalted soul—set the perfect example by personally serving all the pioneering devotees so nicely. Never thinking yourself better than them. Always considering yourself one of the devotees. By your actions teaching all of us for generations to come.

Our aims at ISKCON London remain the same, Śrīla Prabhupāda: Books, *Prasādam,* & Harināma.

While trying to increase and maintain the facility, we are also trying to build a community in central London, an endeavor fraught with obstacles. We are desperate to bring forth the vision and dream you had for London, and we know this will be possible only when you sanction it, Śrīla Prabhupāda. So please continue to bless us. Please help us develop grateful hearts; help us develop simple, sincere hearts that overflow with uninhibited enthusiasm for serving guru and the Vaiṣṇavas. Help us understand what you want in London, and help us to have the full-fledged courage to make it happen, no matter what.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please empower us, your insignificant servants here in London, to bring forth into reality the dream you had for this most important city, the dream that will please your Guru Mahārāja and the entire *guru*-*paramparā*. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us become instruments of your mercy.

We patiently await your mercy while desperately struggling to control our minds and senses, all the while taking shelter of the darśana of Their most beautiful Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara and chanting the holy name.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for giving us this opportunity.

Your servants at ISKCON London, UK.

## London (South)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet, which have traveled across the globe so many times to fulfill the mission of Śrī *Caitanya* Mahāprabhu and your glorious spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day! If not for the sacrifices Your Divine Grace made, there would be no hope for us, the fallen conditioned souls. Now at least there is a golden opportunity for the most unfortunate.

Your family in South London is growing from strength to strength, which shows the power of your preaching. More and more new devotees are being attracted, only due to your blessings. This is going on all over the world, which shows that your blessings are for everyone and not only for the chosen few. You are the personification of the following verse in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.7.36)

*anuvratānāṁ śiṣyāṇāṁ putrāṇāṁ ca dvijottama*  
*anāpṛṣṭam api brūyur guravo dīna-vatsalāḥ*

**“O best among the** ***brāhmaṇas,*** **those who are spiritual masters are very kind to the needy. They are always kind to their followers, disciples, and sons, and without being asked by them, the spiritual master describes all that is knowledge.”**

In the *śāstra* it is also mentioned that the guru should be treated just like Śrī Kṛṣṇa. As Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura wrote in his beautiful *Gurv-aṣṭaka* prayers:

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Krsna\].”**

Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa shows no impartiality, neither do you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your blessings and mercy are there for all to benefit from.

We have been blessed by many enthusiastic souls in South London, and we have been able to start more regular *sat-saṅgas*. Our children’s *saṅga* is really flourishing, and the devotees are able to pass on your teachings to more and more children, who are the future of ISKCON. Our Friday *Bhagavad-gītā* *sat-saṅga* is getting more and more popular, attracting more families to invite devotees into their homes. The youth and *mātājīs’* *saṅgas* have been started, since these groups are also looking for guidance from your teachings. The parents’ workshops are popular, since they need advice from the *śāstra* regarding their duties in bringing up their dependents in Kṛṣṇa consciousness while dealing with all the worldly pressures.

All these *sat-saṅgas* exist only because of the blessings and guidance you are providing through your Bhaktivedanta purports. We are your dependents, and you are perfectly fulfilling the injunction of the following verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (5.5.18):

*gurur na sa syāt sva-jano na sa syāt*  
*pitā na sa syāj jananī na sā syāt*  
*daivaṁ na tat syān na patiś ca sa syān*  
*na mocayed yaḥ samupeta-mṛtyum*

**“One who cannot deliver his dependents from the path of repeated birth and death should never become a spiritual master, a relative, a father, a husband, a mother, or a worshipable demigod.”**

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for spending your precious time translating and writing so many books, especially *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. These books are invaluable for our own guidance as well as our preaching. You shower your blessings on each and every devotee who reads any of your books. Just as chanting the *mahā-mantra* even once can destroy more sins than one can ever commit, so anyone who reads even one of your books will be “hooked” forever on Kṛṣṇa and eventually go back to Godhead.

*praviṣṭaḥ karṇa-randhreṇa svānāṁ bhāva-saroruham*  
*dhunoti śamalaṁ kṛṣṇaḥ salilasya yathā śarat*

**“The sound incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Soul \[i.e.,** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****\], enters into the heart of a self-realized devotee, sits on the lotus flower of his loving relationship, and thus cleanses the dust of material association, such as lust, anger, and hankering. Thus it acts like autumnal rains upon pools of muddy water.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.8.5)**

Recently the devotees from South London had the great opportunity to visit your wonderful Radhadesh temple in Belgium. Truly you have blessed the world by building a house in which the whole world can live. The devotees at Radhadesh were very loving and friendly. We were particularly impressed by the young devotees who were glorifying the Lord by chanting *kīrtana* so nicely and sweetly. We also had the opportunity to have wonderful association and discussions with His Holiness Prahlādānanda Mahārāja and other devotees. The words below from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are very appropriate:

*saṅgamaḥ khalu sādhūnām ubhayeṣāṁ ca sammataḥ*  
*yat-sambhāṣaṇa-sampraśnaḥ sarveṣāṁ vitanoti śam*

**“When there is a congregation of devotees, their discussions, questions, and answers become conclusive to both the speaker and the audience. Thus such a meeting is beneficial for everyone’s real happiness.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **4.22.19)**

Such experiences are due only to your blessings and mercy, for which we are eternally grateful. Please continue to bless us as we further develop the *yātrā* in South London.

*The* servants of your servants at ISKCON South London, UK.

## Malaga

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

And please bless us with your mercy so that we may be able to write this Vyāsa-pūjā offering properly, because without your mercy we are wholly incapable.

Today we celebrate the all-auspicious day of your appearance, the day that the entire universe benefited from, is benefiting from at present, and will continue to benefit from in the future. This day commemorates the day when the light of the spiritual world entered the darkness of the material atmosphere for the benefit of countless living entities who are looking for some sense in a senseless world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have saved us from stupidity, inconsistency, and all inauspiciousness. It is only by your grace that we may gradually understand the truth and taste true peace and happiness. You are our eternal master, father, friend, and shelter. In fact, you are everything for us, and we would be lost without your guidance. How can a blind man find the right path by himself? Impossible! You are the torchlight in the darkness. Whoever sincerely follows you will find the way out of this dark and frightening maze.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day we desire to have as many mouths as Śrī Ananta Śeṣa so that we can describe your glories fully, but unfortunately we are conditioned souls and do not have such capacity. How can an ant describe the grandeur and majesty of the Himalayas? Similarly, how can conditioned souls describe the greatness of a pure devotee of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead?

What you have done and are continuing to do through all those who sincerely strive to follow your footsteps and implement your instructions is not of this world. You are offering us the opportunity to help change the face of the earth, to convert this planet, which is becoming more and more hellish, into a Vaikuṇṭha planet, where the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is recognized, adored and loved as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the friend and well-wisher of everyone.

You are educating and training us not only to elevate ourselves from a state of miserable, crippled consciousness to a state of enlightenment, but also to become qualified to return some day to that place we should never have left, the spiritual realm. What you have done and are doing for us cannot be repaid. Mercy is priceless: it cannot be purchased. How can we repay you for your kindness, your dedication, your gift of knowledge in the service of humanity, your determination, unyielding even in the face of adversity, in the face of the madness of this Age of Kali? How can we match the love you have shown us?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our debt to you is eternal. Thus we simply ask that your compassion for us may result in our becoming your insignificant, humble servants. Physically you have departed, but you are also here, and anyone who strives to serve you with sincerity can feel your presence within their hearts.

You are an ambassador of the spiritual world. Who else would have voluntarily left Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* and come to the lower planets? Please give us your mercy so that we can deeply understand your message, and please empower us to assist you in your mission to save all humanity from animal life and the cycle of *saṁsāra*.

Your eternally grateful servants at ISKCON Malaga, Spain.  
(written by Siddhānta Dāsa)

## Manchester

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most sincere humble obeisances. All glories unto you, our dearmost lord and master!

By the inconceivable mercy and grace of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we have somehow found our way to your lotus feet. You have inspired all of us, both individually and collectively, to find ways to serve your ISKCON mission.

The varieties of people who approach you are diverse, intelligent, subservient, and talented in so many colorful shades, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and their unity of purpose displays variegated beauty. At first this overwhelming diversity can be bewildering because of our previous conditioning. With time, Your Divine Grace carefully guides us to see this spectacular variegatedness as an aspect of the inconceivable wonder of the Supreme Lord.

It is a great credit to your teachings that we have the opportunity to gain a glimpse into the real meaning of the statement “God is great.” With so much sweetness you have revealed the true depth of the greatness of God, Lord Kṛṣṇa. He is an unlimited ocean of pastimes, expansions, beauty, creation, etc. One who understands even a bit of this greatness should dive into the ever-increasing eternal nectar-ocean of loving devotional service. Please grant us everlasting shelter at your lotus feet so that we may surrender our lives in the loving pursuit of carrying on your mission with the heartfelt dedication you and your disciples have taught.

With a population of three million, Manchester is the third largest city in the UK. Among so many souls here, we feel honored to be among the few who have taken the opportunity to render loving service at your lotus feet. Currently our *yātrā* consists of a congregation of more than 130 devotees, ranging from the dedicated to the casual visitor. In 2006 we were just a small group of twenty devotees meeting on Fridays and Sundays, trying to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness responsibly. Even though the temple has been established for over thirty years, it has had limited success establishing a progressive and growing devotee congregation.

ISKCON Manchester is working in close cooperation with the Scottish *yātrā* to support and establish regular Kṛṣṇa conscious activities in northern England. Nāma-haṭṭa groups are active in Bolton, Warrington, Liverpool, Chester, North Wales, Sheffield, Stoke-on-Trent, and Preston. In Manchester we celebrate all the main festivals and have a Ratha-yātrā each year in September. We have inaugurated an annual twelve-hour *kīrtana* festival during Kārtika and have regular monthly *harināmas* in Bolton and Manchester. Currently we are in the process of opening a small storefront preaching center in Stockport, just thirty kilometers from Manchester, led by your dear disciple Jagadātmā Prabhu. We sincerely pray at your lotus feet that you may bless his efforts so that the center can establish roots and further expand your message of Godhead in another town.

In our Manchester *yātrā* we have seventeen initiated disciples, who form the core team of dedicated volunteers. They are *gṛhastha* devotees with children, working and living outside the temple, regularly organizing Kṛṣṇa conscious programs throughout the year. We have many aspiring devotees awaiting the opportunity for initiation; they are all dedicated to the preaching mission. We are deeply grateful to all the congregational devotees, who, searching for your association and mercy, are taking advantage of the International Society of Krishna Consciousness. It is so rare in this world to find sincere devotees, but by dint of your kindness we have this reservoir of sincere devotees regularly associating in ISKCON.

The central desire of all the devotees is that we may become the oasis for all people in northwest England looking to drink from the unlimited ocean of nectar of devotional spiritual enlightenment that ultimately brings the sublime bliss of love of God. Kindly bless us with the intelligence, organizational structure, patience, and tolerance to engage more people in your movement. Kindly send waves of new devotees and empower us to distribute your books and sumptuous *prasādam* and to frequently relish chanting the holy names on *harināma* *saṅkīrtana*. By your mercy may we build, develop, and maintain an ISKCON center in Manchester that daily nourishes the nine processes of devotional service.

We beg always to remain in the mood of your insignificant humble servants and to relish the great opportunity you have afforded us to serve all those who come to quench their thirst in this oasis of nectarean devotional service.

We pray always to be enthusiastically absorbed in your loving shelter.  
Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Manchester, UK.

## Mauritius

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Here in Mauritius our *yātrā* is expanding nicely by your mercy. Under the caring and expert direction of our GBC, His Holiness Bhakti Caitanya Mahārāja, we are seeing ISKCON Mauritius becoming more united and cooperative. On the farm Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma are being very nicely served by the devotees and congregational members. The festivals are being attended by ever-increasing crowds. In February of this year we had an inspiring BBT Africa conference, and we have now printed *On the Way to Kṛṣṇa* for the first time in Mauritian Creole, and our translators are working on your *Bhagavad-gītā* *As It Is*.

We feel we are making progress in implementing your divine plan to make Mauritius the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country in the world. Do you remember when Prime Minister Sir Sewoosagur Ramgoolam wrote to you inviting you to “please come and bless our island with your visit”? You landed at the Plaisance Airport and were escorted off the plane and ushered into the VIP lounge, where you were received by our enthusiastic ISKCON Life Members and supporters Gowtam Teelock and Mr. Ramlallah, who was the editor of the *Mauritius Times*.

The following conversation was recorded by ITV:

Gowtam Teelock: We are very pleased to have you among us, and we welcome you on behalf of the people of Mauritius. Have you had any idea of Mauritius before coming here?

Śrīla Prabhupāda: My idea is to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, because for want of this knowledge our mission of human life is failing all over the world. Therefore I am trying to introduce the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world, without any distinction of caste, creed, or color. God is for everyone. And we have forgotten our relationship with God; therefore we are suffering in so many ways. His instruction is there in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* so if we follow, then we become happy.

When asked about your schedule and programs for the island, you replied,

I want to see the leading men of this place, because if they understand the importance of this movement, then my mission is a success. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* it is said, *yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas tat tad evetaro janaḥ:* “The leaders, whatever they do, the ordinary, common man follows.” So if the leading men of Mauritius, if they understand the importance of this movement, then I am sure it will be very beneficial for everyone.

In the January 1976 issue of the *Back to Godhead* magazine, the following article was published under the title “*Śrīla Prabhupāda* Visits Mauritius”:

A recent tour of Africa took His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda through East and South Africa, and to the island state of Mauritius, six hundred miles off the coast of Madagascar. There he spoke with many people, among them Mr. Khemraj Gungah, president of the Narmadesvara Sivalaya temple in Rose Bell, and Mr. Gautum Teelock, ex–Parliamentary Secretary in the Ministry of Agriculture. Contrasting ancient Vedic culture with modern civilization, His Divine Grace noted:

“Vedic society is based on the principle of love—between husband and wife, father and son, the king and the citizens, and so on. Civilization must be founded on love, not law. By law you cannot make a person love you. First he must love you, then he will gladly follow your law.

“Modern civilization lacks this basis of love, so everything is superficial and false. But if devotion to Kṛṣṇa \[God\] is made the basic principle, then people will be happy.”

Knowing of local plans to reorganize social and political structures, Śrīla Prabhupāda then proposed that an ideal state based on the instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā* be set up in Mauritius. “People will be very inclined to it,” he said. “In a big state—especially a highly industrialized one—it would not be possible, but in a small state like Mauritius you can make it happen. You wouldn’t be making a new experiment. This experiment has already been made and proven successful. In Vedic days, people lived strictly according to the principles summarized by Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. They made the test and proved that the principles are true. So you just have to follow what is already there. You simply have to accept the Vedic authority.”

In your arrival address, Your Divine Grace said,

This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is a very important movement. We are inviting all intelligent persons, the leaders of the society, to understand the basic principle of this movement. If the leaders of the society lead the people in the proper channel on the basic principle of spiritual life, then everything will be solved. Everyone will be happy. These instructions are there in the *Bhagavad-gītā*very elaborately given. Simply we have to follow those instructions. It is not at all difficult. Then all the questions—social problem, political problem, economical problem, religious problem, everything will be nicely solved. We are presenting, therefore, *Bhagavad-gītā* *As It Is*. And you take any question and the answer is there in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* very nicely given. Just like, say, our first problem is an economic problem. So *Bhagavad-gītā* says, “Yes, economic problem will be solved very easily if you produce food grains.” *Annād bhavanti bhūtāni*. You produce food grains. Just like in your country I see there is enough land. If you people produce your food grains—within three months you can get all the necessities of life for the whole year. And the balance time you can save, nine months. And these nine months you can utilize for advancing in spiritual life.

In a class on the *Bhagavad-gītā* you gave on 1 October 1975, Your Divine Grace said:

This rascal havoc has ruined the whole world. Otherwise, if they would have preached *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is, the world situation would have been different. Everything would have been in order, tranquillity, peace, and everybody would have been prosperous. But the rascals will not do that. That is the defect. Therefore our tiny effort is how to present *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is. This is our mission. Even now, in this distorted condition of the world, if we accept *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is, then still, we will be benefited. There is no doubt about it. Therefore our appeal is that throughout the whole world people should come, try to understand *Bhagavad-gītā,* and set up examples and do the needful. Then everything will be peaceful.

This is the peace formula: *bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasāṁ sarva-loka-maheśvaram, suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānam*. *Suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānam*. *Su-hṛt* means “well-wisher.” Well-wisher. Here is the supreme well-wisher. He wants, Kṛṣṇa wants that wherever you live, you live peacefully and happily; and therefore He is called *su-hṛt*. He is desiring everyone’s happiness. Therefore He comes Himself to advise, to instruct how to live, how to follow His instruction, and He leaves these behind Him such books as *Bhagavad-gītā,* and He sends His representative occasionally to revive our consciousness. This business is going on, not only in the human society, even in the animal society. Anywhere, even in demigod society, things are going on like that. Therefore we should know, whatever Bhagavān says, there cannot be any mistake, any illusion, any cheating, or any imperfectness. Then it will be very nice. And that is the fact.

In a press conference held on October 2nd you were asked about ISKCON’s possible political involvement elsewhere, to which you replied:

“Oh, yes. When we are many in number, then we shall take politics also. Yes. First of all let there be perfect men. If there are no perfect men, the government will be imperfect. If there are perfect men, the government will be perfect.

Reporter: Do you think, Swamiji, that man can become perfect?

Prabhupāda: That is the chance of a human being. Therefore in the human society there is school, college, education, culture, not in the animal society, because they can be made into perfect beings, not the animals. So if they are denied proper education, that is the greatest harm. They have got the opportunity for spiritual education, but the authorities are not giving them that opportunity. Reporter: Do you mean the perfectness is to become God or a perfect man? Prabhupāda: You never become God.

Reporter: What does perfectness mean?

Prabhupāda: Perfect means to become godly.

Reporter: Can you be interested directly in politics?

Prabhupāda: Why not? Kṛṣṇa was in politics. So He instructed Arjuna to fight. This is politics—for a good cause. When Arjuna denied that “Kṛṣṇa, I am not willing to kill my, the other side, my brothers and my uncles,” He chastised him that *kutas tvā kaśmalam idaṁ viṣame samupasthitam:* “How you are talking like non-Āryan? What is this nonsense?”.

kutas tvā kaśmalam idaṁ viṣame samupasthitam  
anārya-juṣṭam asvargyam akīrti-karam arjuna

*Anārya. Anārya.* He has said *anārya*. *Ārya* means advanced. *Anārya* means less advanced. *Kṣatriya* means one who gives protection from being hurt, *kṣat. Kṣat* means hurting. So suppose if I unnecessarily hurt you, then it is the duty of the government to give you protection. So unless I am also punished by violence,. he cannot give you protection. So this is also necessary. Therefore in the society there must be *kṣatriya*. The *brāhmaṇas* should be learned; they should give instruction, advice. The *kṣatriyas* should give protection; the *vaiśyas* should produce, economic development; and rest, they should assist—*śūdras,* that’s all. This is the program of Āryan society.

In a morning walk conversation on October 4, Your Divine Grace instructed your disciples:

Government means, nowadays, all rascals. They are elected by rascals and they are rascals. That is the difficulty. Everywhere you go, you will meet only rascals. *Manda*. The definition is given, *manda*. Even in our camp \[ISKCON\] there are so many rascals. Just see the report. Even they have come to be reformed, they are rascals. They cannot give up their rascal habits. Therefore it has been generalized*, manda:* “all bad.” But only difference is that in our camp the bads are being reformed; outside there is no reforming. \[Here\] there is hope of their being good, but outside there is no hope. That is the difference. Otherwise everyone is bad. Without any discrimination you can say. *Mandāḥ sumanda-matayo*. Now, how the government will be good? This is also bad. Mahāprabhu’s name is Patita-pāvana; He is delivering all bad men.

We hope to be able to implement these essential instructions in our lifetime, but it will only be possible by your mercy, so please kindly empower us to do the needful. We are fallen and, it being Kali-yuga, prone to infighting over petty personal ambitions and agendas. So we simply beg you to let us become fixed up in following your directives for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Guru and *Gaurāṅga*.

Your servants at the Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple and ISKCON Vedic Farm, Mauritius.

## Mexico City

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is an opportunity to glorify you and let the world know how great you are. We didn’t know you personally, but we feel we have come to know you through your books. Your books save lives. Hardly any other book can do that. Furthermore, your books save souls. Every line of every page contains the most complete and refreshing message. No other books are like that. Everything about you inspires us to serve Kṛṣṇa, to meditate on Him. Any picture of you, or any audio or video recording of you, is a kind of refreshing rain shower for the heart. We know that you said we can know you through your books, but one of the reasons we engage in your service is the hope to see you face to face some day.

We would like to tell you many stories about *saṅkīrtana,* preaching, and service and see your big smile and wide-open eyes. We are feeling only one percent of what your disciples broadcast when they talk about you, and our hearts melt with that small glimpse of your glory. Your potency is present in all your temples and all your books. Serving your mission is the greatest feeling we have ever had. Please let us transmit that feeling to all the people in the world. That bliss comes from you.

Let us serve you more and more. This is the only thing we need.

We pray to Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Madana-Gopāla to give us the intelligence and skill to let the world know how great you are.

Your granddisciples at the Śrī Śrī RādhāMadana-Gopāla temple in Mexico City, Mexico.

## Miami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

One of your most prominent Vaiṣṇava qualities is your deep humility. Although it is an undisputed fact that Lord Caitanya empowered you to spread His *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world, you always remained completely humble, giving all credit to your Guru Mahārāja. You never became overwhelmed, bewildered, or puffed up with false pride, even when you had thousands of disciples who were ready to do whatever you wanted and when your worldwide ISKCON movement became prominent, with many transcendental books, temples, farms, schools, restaurants, and more. This humility was quite natural for you because, as a pure devotee of the Lord, you fully realized that Lord Kṛṣṇa was the ultimate controller and enjoyer.

It was only out of profound humility that you fully surrendered to the order of your spiritual master and took the risk of going to America to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Once you were here, it was out of humility that you tolerated all kinds of difficulties and challenges, preaching to the ignorant lost souls of Kali-yuga. You did not come to the West just to return to India and become famous as a preacher who had converted some Westerners to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Accepting as your life and soul your spiritual master’s order to give the Lord’s mercy to everyone, you simply tried to carry it out.

It was an open secret that your spiritual master wanted his disciples to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western world. Even if you had preached to a only few people in America and then returned to India, you would have followed his instruction. You understood, however, that the deepest desire of your spiritual master was not just to make a superficial presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to a handful of people and then leave. His desire was that you firmly establish Lord Caitanya’s mission in the Western world.
